MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-01-10 | CLIP_ID: 833 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 833

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 10, 2022 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center, 510 W. 20th St., and via electronic conference meeting.  
Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan 
Cook, Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, 
Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance 
was recited. 
 
 Election of City Council President for 2022.   Mayor Collins announced that pursuant to 
Title 2 of Cheyenne City Code the Mayor conducts the election for the office of City Council 
President but does not cast a vote.  Mr. White nominated Mr. Roybal, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. 
Roybal accepted the nomination.  There being no further nominations, Dr. Aldrich  moved to cease 
nominations, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Roybal as City Coun cil President for 2022 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Collins presented a plaque to 
Mr. White in appreciation of his time as Council President for 2021.   
 
 Election of City Council Vice-President for 2022.  Mayor Collins announced that pursuant 
to Title 2 of Cheyenne City Code the Mayor conducts the election for the office of City Council 
Vice-President but does not cast a vote.  Mr.  Cook nominated Mr. Johnson, seconded by Mr.  
Roybal.  Mr. Johnson accepted the nomination.  There being no further nominations, Dr. Aldrich 
moved to cease nominations, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Johnson as City Council Vice-President 
for 2022 carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda .  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve, seco nded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 28, 2021.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create the definitions, ‘Functional Turf’ and ‘Non -Functional Turf’ and 
amending various sections of Article 6 .  Brad Brooks, Director of Board of Public Utilities  
(BOPU), expressed BOPU’s  overall support of the  ordinance and expressed appreciation to 
Planning and Development and BOPU staff who have worked on the ordinance.  Mr. Brooks 
provided information on the amount of water the city is permitted to utilize and  water sources 
noting the ordinance change is proactive to preserve water.  Mr. Brooks further provided 
information on several proposed projects that will result in additional water including:  enlarging 
water reservoirs, optimization of snake system, recycled water system expansion , Belvoir well 
field expansion, well field ground penetration project (map of possible groundwater) and utilizing 
recycled waste water which will free up water sources.  Mr. Brooks advised it is estimated that the 
city can probably survive for 3 years on the current water supply if no additional water was to be 
received.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr . Johnson .  Appreciation was expressed to Mr. Brooks for his 
overview of the city’s water supply and comments were made on the following: importance of 
attractive landscaping requirements for businesses, importance of ongoing water conserv ation 
efforts, businesses still being able to have bluegrass if they wish and the importance of Planning 
and Development staff working with developers and the community to help them understand the 
definition of functional vs. non -functional turf.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#4409)        
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development – 
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD - Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, Amending 
Ordinance No. 4232 by modifying certain standards of the Adopted District, Planned Unit 
Development (PUD), for a portion of Sections 8 & 9, all of the W1/2 Sect ion 14, a portion of the 
E ½ E ½ of Section 17, and all of Sections 15, 16, & 21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of 
the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming  (Located to the southeast of the intersection of E. College 
Drive and Avenue C) .  Casey Palma, Palm a Land Planning, agent for the owner, provided 
information on the Sweetgrass PUD that was approved in 2018 with certain requirements noting 
the proposed development area has 1,400 sq. ft. of open space. He advised the owner looked at the 
PUD and what might be discouraging and certain provisions seemed excessive.  When there is a 
parcel in a PUD which is contiguous with  the Greenway, some of the burden can be reduced to 
provide affordable housing.   Upon inquiry, Mr. Palma provided informat ion on the requested 
proposed change to the PUD which includes 40% contiguity to the Greenway and does not require 
a clubhouse in the development noting this doesn’t water down code requirements but is a balance 
for the need of providing affordable housing with the open space and connectivity to the Greenway.  
Upon further inquiry, Mr. Palma explained th e proposed  change wouldn’t just affect this 
development because it is a change to the PUD in its entirety and only certain properties meet the 
requirement o f 40% contiguity to Greenway.  Upon further inquiry , Mr. Palma explained th e 
ordinance change isn’t for the development construction piece which is reviewed through the site 
plan process.  In response to a comment made regarding commitment of the development being 
affordable housing, Mr. Palma advised as agent of  the owner and representing HOA entities, it 
isn’t something they necessarily have complete control over  and the ordinance change allows for 
increased density.  He further advised this is a way to reduce costs for the developer which enables 
them to provide more density and lower costs for renters.  Mr. Palma explained upon inquiry, that 
proper notification was made to adjacent properties related to Greenway access , open space, etc. 
and advised the proposed change does not reduce the overall open space in Sweetgrass.  Mr. Palma 
provided further project information including:  Greenway and trails plan in  the PUD, project is 
within 1,000 ft. of green space in commercial area , the parcel has direct access through a tunnel 
being constructed  to LCCC and the demographic for the units will likely  be students  and as 
Sweetgrass moves south, there’s less contiguity to greenway and burden would be higher to find 
40% contiguity.   Upon inquiry, Connor White, Planning and Development Department, explained 
that in the past 2 years developments with 100 units have been very minimal, and generally they 
see 80-90 unit developments.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. White. Upon inquiry, Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, explained 
the scope of the Sweetgrass development seems to change, but currently there are 3-4 lots that can 
support 100 -unit developments. Comments were made on the following: opposition of the 
ordinance change as it will apply to other properties and developments not just Sweetgrass, staff’s 
original recommendation of  25 units wi th 40% contigu ity to the G reenway, large apartment 
complexes typically include a clubhouse, how the proposed ordinance change could push people 
into city parks which could burden city employees to maintain increased park population and 
concern that there is no guarantee the development will be affordable housing.    Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  
Voting “no” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Collins and Mr. Segrave. (#4410)  
        
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4396 Defining the Terms and 
Conditions of Employment for Sworn Officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 
2021-2022.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr . White.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend  by substitute Exhibit B which 
includes the $1.50 wage increase approved at the December 28,  2021 meeting of the gov erning 
body, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend by substitute Exhibit B carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4411) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.76, Abandoned Vehicles, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Chapter 8.60, Nuisances, of Title 8, Health 
and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes ” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Chapter 6.08.200, Keeping of Bees, of 
Chapter 6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Johnson explained there are no changes from when the state updated their 
requirements in 2015, the amendment just moves the section from Nuisance to Animals in the city 
code.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the Zoning Classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis for land located northwest of the intersection of Nationway and Logan Avenue 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2022 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §16 -4-112 & §16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6205)  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Plan One Architects, 
for the Emergency Response Facility design and construction Administration Services Project 
(Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$752,580.00). (#7465) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between WYDOT and the Cheyenne MPO for the Transit 
Connection Feasibility Analysis: Northern Colorado to Southern Wyoming.  Dale Steenbergen, 
President/CEO of the Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, spoke in support of the project  
noting the importance of studying transportation along the front range. Mr. Steenbergen advised 
there are a few transportation projects for the front range in the works including Bustang (bus that 
currently goes from Ft. Collins to Denver) and the possibility of a front range rail.  Mr. Steenbergen 
noted the MOU and analysis will be pivotal for transportation between Wyoming and Colorado.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an amount of $43,108.85, seconded by Mr . Johnson.  Concern 
was expressed that with increased options of transportation on the front range, we may be 
incentivizing people to live in Colorado and work in Wyoming.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Esquibel voting “no”. (#7466) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization (MPO) and HDR Engineering, Inc., for the Transit Connection Feasibility 
Analysis:  Northern Colorado to Southern Wyoming .  Tom Mason, MPO Director, advised th e 
agreement is for the Wyoming portion of the study  and that transportation choices will vary.  He 
noted the study is for transit feasibility between Cheyenne and northern Colorado. Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Mason advised the study will take approximately 1 year and w ill include determining how 
many people in the community currently commute daily and will determine if there is a demand 
for van pooling or to extend the Bustang route which currently ends at  the Harmony/1-25 
interchange.  Mr. Roybal advised there was no r ecommendation from the Public Services 
Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an amount of $86,217.70, seconded by Mr.  White.  
It was expressed that it is believed our community  has experts to provide the study services.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. 
Esquibel voting “no”. (#7467) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-9-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Sustainable 
Modular Management, for the Compost Facility Prefabricated Metal Office Trailer Project (Solid 
Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $118,794.00). 
(#7468) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-7-22 for furnishing fourteen (14) new four-door 2022-2023 model 
utility (SUV) Police Interceptors, for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department (2019 -
2022 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Duval Ford/Dana 
Safety Supply, Jacksonville, FL and Livingston, MT, in an amount not to exceed $839,510.10). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-22-21 for furnishing One (1) ¾ ton crew cab truck for the Traffic 
Division, one (1) ¾ ton regular cab truck for the Facilities Maintenance Division, and three (3) ¾ 
ton crew cab trucks for the Street and Alley Division (2015 -2018 & 2019 -2022 1% Sales Tax) .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Spradley Barr Motors d/b/a Ken Garff , 
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $237,040.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Motorola 
Solutions/Bearcom, for the purchase of Fourteen (14) Motorola APX8500 Radios for the City of 
Cheyenne Police Department (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(in an amount not to exceed $60,192.83). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-9-22 for furnishing three (3) new multi-purpose response vehicles 
(type 1 pumpers) and one (1) 100’ heavy duty aerial tower, for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne 
Fire & Rescue Department (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.   
                
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
  
 Appointment of  Jeffrey Storey to the Airport Board.  Mr.  Roybal moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King Jr. holiday on Monday, 
January 17, 2022, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held as 
follows: Finance Committee will meet at Noon on Tuesday, January 18, 2022 and Public Services 
Committee will meet at Noon on Wednesday, January 19, 2022 at the Kiwanis Community House 
at 4603 Lions Park Drive, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting. 
  
 Under other business:   
  
 Bill Gonzales, Street and Alley Division, requested equitable benefits across the board for 
City employees when there is a snow related closure.  He explained his division is responsible for 
reporting to work for snow removal and there is no compensation for the work performed.  Mayor 
Collins expressed appreciation to Mr. Gonzales and his co-workers for their services. 
 
 Mr. Johnson noted the governing body  goal setting session will be held on Saturday, 
January 15th, and that Mr. Gonzales’ request is a planned topic of discussion.   
 
 Dr. Aldrich advised a Ward III Council and Coffee will be held this Saturday, January 15th, 
from 8 a.m. to 10 a.m. at Dazbog Coffee, 3911 E. Pershing Blvd.  She also invited members of the 
governing body to attend the event “In Her Shoes” that will be held January 11-January 13th at 
the Kiwanis Community House, 4603 Lions Park Drive .  She reported Safehouse hosts the role 
playing session and attendees participate as being in domestic violence relationships. 
 
 Mr. White thanked his City Council colleagues for a successful year and wished Mr. 
Roybal well in his upcoming Council Presidency.  He also advised he is looking forward to the 
upcoming governing body goal setting session  noting it was very helpful last year.  He further 
expressed appreciation to the public, members of the governing body and city staff and particularly 
Jennifer McClelland, Executive Assistant to the City Council.   
 
 Mayor Collins advised the January 24,  2022 City Council meeting will be held at  Storey 
Gym, 2811 House Avenue beginning at 6:00 p.m. and thanked the Civic Center staff for use of the 
facility for the meeting.   
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:05 p.m.   
 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
  
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  January 20, 2022

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-01-24 | CLIP_ID: 834 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 834

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 24, 2022 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the desi gnation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda .  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 10, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 10.76, Abandoned Vehicles, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.    
Comments were made in support of the ordinance and appreciat ion was expressed to Captain 
David Janes, Police Department, for his efforts on the ordinance.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. (#4412) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 8.60, Nuisances, of Title 8, Health 
and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. (#4413) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 6.08.200, Keeping of Bees, of 
Chapter 6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4414) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the Zoning Classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis for land located northwest of the intersection of Nationway and Logan Avenue.  
Reporting fo r Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Charles Bloom, Director of the Planning & Development 
Department, provided information on the proposed development and advised the Planning 
Commission recommended approval.  Mr. Bloom reported the development involves a demolition 
and rebuild of the current dog grooming business  currently on the property.  Upon inquiry, Eric 
Fountain, Director of the Compliance Department, explained vehicles adjacent to  the property 
have temporary registration and 2 shipping containers of junk have been removed from the area.   
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Johnson)  
 
 RESOLUTION –Establishing and adopting a Parking Fine Schedule for parking fees 
pursuant to City Code Section 10.60.040.  Dr. Aldrich advised the item was reviewed under other 
items for the agenda at the January 18, 2022 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, provisions of 
Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Dr. Aldrich moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “ yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#6206) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing up to a $14,890,000 interfund loan from the One Percent 
Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to the Special Purpose Option Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to 
facilitate the construction of three fire stations, purchase fire trucks and replace the cemetery 
irrigation system as approved by voters on the November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant under the Staffing for Adequate Fire and Emergency Response (SAFER) 
Program, on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of adding nine 
new firefighter positions – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying Planning Commission Action amending the Future Land Use 
Maps of PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Community Business to Mixed -Use 
Commercial for property generally located west of Campbell Avenue, north of Nationway, and 
south of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Cook) 
 
[CA] Grant of Right-of-Way Easement on City Owned Property, per Exhibit A, between the City 
of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power Company for a 150’ wide right -of-way and 
easement for construction and maintenance of overhead power facilities – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between City of Cheyenne and Doran Lummis to 
authorize filing of platting applications(s) for City-owned lands for the purpose of access to future 
development – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6957 between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming 
Cattle Co., LLC adding the Mariah Pasture and extending the term of the Belvoir Ranch Grazing 
Lease Agreement through December 31, 2024 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6441 between the City of Cheyenne and Microsoft 
Corporation for the renewal to the City’s Enterprise Office 365 Online Enrollment – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-9-22 for furnishing three (3) new multi-purpose response vehicles 
(type 1 pumpers) and one (1) 100’ heavy duty aerial tower, for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne 
Fire & Rescue Department.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Front Range 
Fire Apparatus, Frederick, Colorado, in an amount not to exceed $3,740,216.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-7-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and S&S 
Builders, LLC, for the Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs and Door Repairs 
Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.   
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-8-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Front 
Range Roofing Systems, LLC, for the Ice and Events Center Roof Replacement Project (General 
Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and KONE, Inc., for the repair 
of the South East  Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking Garage (General Fund 
Reserves) – referred to Finance Committee. 
               
 Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Robin Lockman advised the contract related to the Knife 
River voucher would be provided to members of the governing body.  No further comments were 
made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement of a Public Hearing was made to be held February 14, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditi ons required by 
Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 521 Southwest Drive (located east of Southwest Drive, south 
of I-80). 
   
 Under other business:   
 
 Chris Layman expressed concerns relating to  the 26th Street construction project which 
included: deep open holes , mud, pedestrian difficulty navigating the area , delay of project 
completion, safety hazards and major inconvenience for the last 8 months.   Ms. Layman advised 
she was given a time frame in which the project would be completed by the construction manager, 
noting that time frame has since past.   Mayor Collins noted it was anticipated the entire project 
would be completed sooner and all underground work has been done.  The remainder of the project, 
including paving the road , can’t happen until the weather improves.  Ms. Layman advised the 
project engineer assured her the hole would be back filled and graded prior to being paved, and 
she requested this be done.  Mayor Collins advised he would check with the company to see if this 
can be completed. 
  
  Jim Ridgeway provided photos of the 26th Street construction project area and voice d 
concerns with the project which included: open trenches, equipment and culverts piled up  and 
fence posts being utilized  incorrectly all of which are safety hazards .  Mr. Ridgeway noted 
additional citywide concerns involving construction signage which alludes to work being done in 
areas where no work is actually be done  and requested signs be removed. Mayor Collins advised 
he would look into Mr. Ridgeway’s concerns.   Upon inquiry, City Engineer Tom Cobb advised 
AVI is the construction manager for the 26th Street project.   
 
 Casey Quinn, BluePeak Fiberoptic Internet, provided information on the company noting 
they began fiberoptic internet construction locally in October.  He provided contact information 
and advised any constituent issues or concerns can be directed to him. 
 
 Dr. Aldrich advised the City Council is challenging the Laramie County Commission ers 
during the Boys and Girls Club Chili Challenge fundraiser which will be held on Friday, January 
28, 2022, from 5-8 p.m. at Little America. 
     
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:30 p.m.   
 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
  
        Kylie Soden,   
Deputy City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  January 30, 2022

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-02-14 | CLIP_ID: 836 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 836

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 14, 2022 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel 
(via Zoom) , Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal and Jeff White.   
Absent: Mr. Cook and Mr. Segrave.   Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 PROCLAMATION – Designating February  12-19, 2022 as Entrepreneurship Week.   
Mayor Collins read the proclamation into the record and expressed appreciation to members of the 
Innovation and Entrepreneur Advisory Council who were in attendance. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #25(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal, 
agenda item # 17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Johnson moved to approve  
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 24, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 521 Southwest Drive (located east of 
Southwest Drive, south of I -80).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Depar tment, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd noted the affiliated annexation and zone change ordinances 
are also on the agenda.  Upon request, Mr. Llo yd provided examples of developments for the 
Mixed-Use Employment zone category.   There being no further comments or input  and purpose

 RESOLUTION – Authorizing up to a $14,890,000 interfund loan from the One Percent 
Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to the Special Purpose Option Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to 
facilitate the construction of three fire stations, purchase fire trucks and replace the cemetery 
irrigation system as approved by voters on the November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot.   Jim 
Ridgeway advised he is in support of the project and inquired as to when construction would take 
place.  John Kopper, Chief of the Fire Department, advised the city has a Professional Services 
Agreement with Plan One Architects and the projects are almost shovel ready, estimating the first 
fire station to be ready for construction by June .  Upon inquiry, Chief Kopper provided the 
following information: the public areas of the fire stations will meet ADA requirements, each staff 
member on duty will have their own bedroom in the new fire stations and there will be 3 separate 
bathrooms.  County Commissioner Gunner Malm expressed appreciation to Mayor Colli ns and 
the governing body for looking at this funding option, noting it is a great opportunity to save 
taxpayers money on the projects  and to everyone who provided information to the public on the 
6th Penny projects as they are instrumental in the community.  Mayor Collins advised that due to 
inflation, the cost will continue to rise for construction of the fire  stations and this approach 
provides for financial responsibility and accountability to the voters.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Concerns and comments  were made relating to the Consent Agenda 
process regarding public comment options.  City Treasurer Robin Lockman, upon inquiry, advised 
this type of funding option was utilized in the past for construction of the David R. Romero Park.  
Ms. Lockman further advised  the funds come  out of 5 th Penny fund, provided information on 
available funding in the 5 th Penny fund  and noted it will not affect reserves .  Mayor Collins 
explained utilizing this funding option includes repayment to the fund with interest.  Appreciation 
was expressed to Ms. Lockman and her team for their outstanding services and their assistance 
with the resolution.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
(#6207)

 Under other business:   
 
 Upon inquiry by Mayor Collins,  City Clerk Kris Jones advised the following item was 
being introduced for the purpose of being referred to Finance Committee to be held on February 
22, 2022 at the Kiwanis Community House:  Contract between the City of Cheyenne and the 
Comea Shelter granting a subaward to be used to increase available emergency housing and

 Jim Ridgeway expressed concern relating to residential sewer fees being billed for 35 day 
periods and how the extra days affect the budgeted bill amount.  Mr. Ridgeway advised the Board 
of Public Utilities explain ed the late reading s were due to staffing issues within Zone 17 .  Mr. 
Ridgeway noted he does not believe the consumer should have to pay for the inefficiencies.  Mayor 
Collins advised water and sewer rates are established during the winter months.  Mr. Ridgeway 
provided photos and information regarding Lions Park walking paths and advised there are issues 
with standing water  and drainage problems which freezes and causes safety hazards.  Mr. 
Ridgeway inquired about the Bird  Scooters and requirements of the company relating to picking 
up abandon scooters.  Mayor Collins advised users can leave them in areas around town and the 
company is responsible  for retrieving and maintain ing them.  Mr. Johnson provided detailed 
information regarding reporting concerns involving the scooters and advised  residents can find 
additional information on the city’s and Bird Rides’ websites.  Mr. Johnson provided additional 
information relating to violations of city code involving the scooters.  Mr. R idgeway advised he 
believes it is a burden on citizens to contact the company.  Compliance Director Eric Fountain, 
upon inquiry, reported an an nual review will be held soon to address any issues involving the 
scooters.  Deputy Community Recreation and Events Director Jason Sanchez, upon inquiry , 
provided information relating to snow removal  in Lions Parks  and advised he would look into 
drainage concerns.  Mr. Ridgeway further referenced a memo provided by City Treasurer Robin 
Lockman on January 18 th related to  the resolution authorizing the interfund loan for various 
November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot projects and the funding of the 2019-2022 5th Penny ballot, 
noting overages collected that could be allotted for other needed improvements. 
 
 Dr. Aldrich advised there will be a City Council Ward III Council and Coffee event on 
Saturday, February 19th, from 9 am - 11 am at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing Blvd.).  
 
 Mr. Johnson commended colleagues for their attendance and participation at the annual  
Boys & Girls Club Chili Cook and expressed appreciation to Triumph High Culinary Club for 
their 3rd Place win.  Mayor Collins advised the fundraising event included a friendly comp etition 
between members of the Governing Body and the County Commissioners.  Dr.   Aldrich reported 
the event raised almost $20k between the fundraising efforts of the Governing Body and the 
County Commissioners. 
 
Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16 -4-
405(a)(x), seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.  Mayor Collins advised the executive session will be held to discuss the upcoming 
annual Fire Union contract and announced members would meet in executive session, not open to 
the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, City Treasurer, Chief of Staff and Fire Chief 
be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the 
executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close 
of the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 7:02 p.m.).     
 
 Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:58 p.m. 
 
 
 There being  no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:58 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
  
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-02-28 | CLIP_ID: 838 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 838

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 28, 2022 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #21 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item 
#26(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 14, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
 Mayor Collins opened a public  hearing for renewal applications for existing City liquor 
licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite 
distillery, special malt beverage and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  There 
being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met , Mayor Collins closed 
the public hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership and location of retail 
liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas 
limited liability company d/b/a King Soopers, 3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(license currently held by Avanti, Inc., d/b/a Dell Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Kevin Georges expressed concern regarding the proposed transfer of the 
license to a major corporation and recent prior retail liquor license transfers that have went to large 
corporations.  Mr. Georges expressed the need of smaller local businesses having the opportunity 
to obtain retail liquor licenses. Matt Kim-Miller, attorney with Holland & Hart, LLP (applicant’s 
representative), provided information on the request noting the transfer involves a long-standing 
package liquor store location which will be expanded, noting he believes the location is appropriate 
for continued package liquor s ervices for the community.   Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones 
explained the license was originally issued several years ago and th e request is for a transfer of 
location and ownership and that the additional retail liquor license awarded to the City based on 
the 2020 census  has not been awarded yet .  Comment was made regarding the lack of liquor 
licensing options in the state.   Upon further inquiry, Ms. Jones advised it was her understanding 
that King Soopers will expand the existing package liquor location and open the access from the 
King Soopers store into the current liquor store location.  Mr. Kim-Miller confirmed the plan is to 
expand the existing liquor store space into the prior Hallmark location and there will be a doorway 
into King Soopers where point of sale will occur. Upon inquiry, Mr. Kim -Miller advised the 
proposal is to have doors between the grocery and liquor spaces.  There being no further comments 
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and 
referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent 
to enter into a contract with Comea Shelter to grant a subaward from Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Funds to be used to increase available emergency housing and temporary shelter 
for Cheyenne’s homeless population .  There being no  comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated February 17, 2022, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Brad Brooks, 
Director of Board of Public Utilities , provided information on the proposed substitute noting the 
language changes in sections 13.04.050 and 13.04.120.   Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4416) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5 -1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Dis tricts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses .  Brenda B irkle and Dan Dorsch , 
Affordable Housing Task Force , spoke in support of the ordinance noting it will contribute to 
affordable housing in the community .  Charles Bloom, Director of Plan ning and Development,  
provided information on  a request to include a change for “Entertainment, Amusement, 
Recreation-Other” from a Conditional Use to a Permitted use in the CBD zoning district noting 
the change would remove the requirement for these types  of businesses in the downtown area to 
go before the Board of Adjustment for approval resulting in being able to go into business sooner.  
Mr. Bloom advised  staff has reviewed  the change request  and believes it is appropriate for  the 
downtown CBD zone.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend Attachment “A” to change 
“Entertainment, Amusement, Recreation-Other” from a Conditional Use to a Permitted Use in the 
CBD zoning district, seconded by Mr . Segrave .  Upon inquiry related to a scenario where an 
outdoor shooting range business could potentially open a business in the downtown area without 
notification, Mr. Bloom and Mr. Johnson provided information on existing ordinances that would 
prevent that type of business in the downtown area.   Motion to amend by Attachment A carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended car ried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint .  Mr. Cook moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute 
dated February 24, 2022, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Charles Bloom , Director of Planning and 
Development, provided information on the substitute noting the acreage was changed from 100 to 
80 for when developments can submit a common landscape plan.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I -80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I -80.  Mr.  Cook moved to approv e on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Busine ss to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circl e.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Cook confirmed the zone change request is to allow for outdoor storage.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and 
Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  
Mayor Collins announced there would be a 3 -minute time limit for speakers and that comments 
and discussion should remain cordial. Speaking in opposition of the ordinance were: Robin 
Goodspeed, Eric Crosby, Jay Meyer, Andrew Dimit, Steven Edgar Olson, Nick Dawson, Alicia 
Green, Bob Wilson, Steve Milliad, Matt Freeman, Angelina Freeman, Sandra Cane and Mark   
Moss.  Topics discussed included: existing statutes, ordinances and policies that address bullying, 
harassment and violence; potential of ordinance being harmful towards individuals with opposing 
beliefs; affect the ordinance may have on re ligious groups; national events relating to similar 
ordinances in other states; confusion and challenges relating to the ordinance; biblical beliefs; how 
reporting offences to the Police Department will be handled; concern that non -supporters of the 
ordinance don’t support the protected groups outlined in the ordinance; belief that Cheyenne is not 
in need of the ordinance; suggestion to approach harassment concerns in other ways and request 
to allow more community input related to the ordinance.  Speaking in support of the ordinance 
were: Amber L eyba and Dale Steenbergen,  Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce , Troy 
Rumpf, Stuart Campbell and Keil Bergeron.  Topics discussed included: ordinance importance 
relating to economic development; witnessed threats and slurs; importance of ordinance as a 
statement of support for the protected groups and the need for the ordinance.  Additional comments 
were made that some companies have concerns related to locating in Cheyenne and recruitment as 
there are perceptions that issues and biased attitudes exist locally, and their desire is for every staff 
member to be treated with respect.  It was also note d that some military members stationed here 
have experienced harassment by local  businesses.  It was a cknowledged that laws exist on the 
federal level and opposing views were voiced on enforcement of those laws.  Reporting for 
Committee of the Whole, Mr. R oybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . 
Johnson.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by adding “political affiliation” to the appropriate sections 
throughout the ordinance that reference the protected classes, s econded by Mr. Johnson.  Dr. 
Aldrich advised the purpose of th e amendment is due to past physical attacks and vandalism at 
local political party’s headquarters.  It was noted other states have included political affiliation in 
similar ordinances.  Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk , advised it would be appropriate to 
include “political affiliation” to other sections in the ordinance for clarification of referenced 
protected groups and noted that was the intention of the amendment.  Motion to amend carried .  
Voting “yes” all members of the governing body.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend Section 9.08.120 
by removing Section A -3.  Motion died due to lack of a second.   Dr. Aldrich moved to amend 
Section 9.08.120 by adding a new Section E  – “This Ordinance will be considered null and void 
when applied to citizens in legitimate exercise of rights enumerated under Article 1, Sections 2, 3, 
20, and 36 of the Wyoming Constitution and the First Amendment of the United State s 
Constitution.  Motion died due to lack of a second.   References were made to previous Supreme 
Court cases involving const itutional protected class cases, some of which involved religious 
affiliations and beliefs .  Appreciation was expressed to everyone who has put effort into the 
ordinance, and it was further noted that everyone in the community should be protected.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, 
Cemeteries, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne 
pertaining to Cemetery fees .  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . 
Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public District to CB Community Business 
for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30 – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Investment Policy 
Statement.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6210) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2023 and 2024, July 1, 2022 
to June 30, 2024.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6211)  
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 
of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6 th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive west and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive) .  
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning, agent for the owner, advised a replat was previously planned 
for a multi-family parcel in  the S weetgrass PUD.  He provided details of the Sweetgrass 
development history noting the family who owned the property supported multi -family 
development and explained that in general the Sweetgrass development has been decades in the 
planning.  Mr. Palma advised the master plan included a 7.68 acre plat for 168 units which is now 
for 116 units which is within the provisions of PlanCheyenne for urban residential stipulations and 
noted the exception is the PUD is unique.  He provided additional information on proposed open 
space, land reserved for public schools and public parks.  Comments and concerns were raised 
regarding proposed density of the project and related anticipated reduction of street infrastructure.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Palma reported it is anticipated there will be many additional filings relating to 
the continued development of Sweetgrass which will take years to complete.  Following concerns 
and comments regarding setting a precedence on high density and size requirements with the plat, 
Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development,  advised that every plat filed will be 
considered on its own.  Comment was made that the proposed plat is fully compliant with the PUD 
as it is written today.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Concern was expressed 
relating to 400 people residing in the vicinity if the final plat is  approved, possible issues with 
homeowner’s associations relating to maintenance and the challenge to provide adequate parking.  
Motion failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” - Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Roybal . Voting “no” - Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn , Mr. Segrave and Mr. 
White.  
 
 RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee schedule for Planning and Development projects . Brad 
Emmons, AVI, expressed appreciation to Planning & Development Director  Charles Bloom for 
his efforts on  the resolution.  Mr. Emmons voiced concern regarding the proposed revised fee 
schedule and the proposed requirement to obtain a property appraisal for right -of-way vacations, 
as currently appraisals are taking approximately 8 weeks to complete.  Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr . Esquibel .  Mr. White moved to amend Attachment “A” by substitute dated 
February 22, 2022, seconded by Mr.  Esquibel.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, provided details for the proposed substitut e which includes a change to the street 
vacation fee and noted it is up to the governing body if they choose to require the proposed 
appraisal requirement and fee.   Motion to amend Attachment “A” carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.   Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom provided information on the $50.00 
permit fee explaining it is due to the amount of Planning and Development staff time required to 
perform reviews of numerous permits.  He noted the proposed fee would generate approximately 
$23,000.00 annua lly.  John Edwards , Saddle Ridge Sub division, expressed concern on fee 
increases that will have an effect on affordable housing.  Mr. Bloom provided information on staff 
review time on the different types of plats submitted and requested consideration to r equire a 
higher fee for expedited plats as opposed to preliminary and final plats.  Discussion topics included 
an option of capping the price per lot to $2,500.00, desire to avoid deterring development and 
option to postpone for additional discussion and review.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to March 
14, 2022, seconded by  Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne raise d a point of order regarding the motion to 
postpone as it was made on top of the motion to amend.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney S tefanie 
Boster advised the motion to amend could either be approved or denied prior to postponement and 
City Clerk Kris Jones advised the motion to postpone was out of order.  Mayor Collins ruled the 
motion to postpone out of order and called for a vote on the motion to amend.  Motion to amend 
failed.  Voting “no”– all members of the governing body.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to March 
14, 2022, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. 
 
 RESOLUTION – For Gov erning Body approval of a one -year Bronze Boot Sponsor 
relationship between Cheyenne Frontier Days and Miller/Coors for 202 2.  Mr. White moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr . Esquibel.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated February 23,

sponsorship type due to incorrect information originally submitted to the Clerk’s office.  Motion 
to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6212)

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-03-14 | CLIP_ID: 840 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 840

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 14, 2022 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel (arriving at 7:35 via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda . Upon request by  Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #20 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Johnson, 
agenda item #28(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request by  Mr. Segrave, and 
pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson, agenda item #28(d) was removed 
from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 28, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on March 3, 2 022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5 -1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, 
Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4417) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint .  Mr. Cook moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to 
March 28, 2022, seconded by Mr.  Segrave. Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, advised discussions had occurred with individuals that had concerns and they would 
like to prepare amendments  to address those concerns.  Mr. Bloom noted there w ill likely be an 
additional postponement requested.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I -80.  Mr . Cook moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. (#4418) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. (#4419) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circle.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#4420) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, S exual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, or Political Affiliation of Chapter 9.08, Offenses 
Against Public Peace and Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mayor Collins  announced there would be a 3 -minute time limit for 
speakers.  Speaking in opposition of the ordinance were: Bob Wilson, Tom Perea, Steve Milliad, 
John Romero Martinez and Lynn Hutchings.  Topics discussed included: existing statutes , 
ordinances and policies that address bullying, harassment and violence; potential of ordinance 
being harmful towards individuals with opposing beliefs and being used as a wedge; confusion 
and challenges relating to the ordinance; biblical beliefs; how re porting offences to the Police 
Department will be handled; belief that Cheyenne is not in need of the ordinance; the need for the 
community to come together to address malicious harassment issues without an ordinance  and 
civil rights.  Speaking in support of the ordinance were: Amber Leyba and Dale Steenbergen, 
Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, James Peebles, Dr. Larry Meuli, and Sarah Burlingame, 
Director of Wyoming Equality .  Topics discussed included: Governing Body support for the 
ordinance; ordinance importance relating to economic development;  anti-lynching senate bill; 
issues in Ukraine; importance of ordinance to protect children and military lives; existing 
harassment and discrimination in the community; city’s poor economic development ranking, and 
the ordinance contributing to everyone feeling safe and supported .  Additional comments were 
made related to supporting the spirit of the ordinance but opposition to the way the ordinance is 
written.  Dr. Aldrich read a statement related to a newspape r editorial and apologized for 
misspeaking.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. (#4421) 
 
 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, Cemeteries, 
of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne pertaining 
to Cemetery fees.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr . Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4422)

repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use

for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30 .  
Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting

 RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee  schedule for Planning and Development projects . 
(Postponed from February 28, 2022).  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. 
White moved to amend  by substitute dated March 4, 2022, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Charles 
Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, provided information on the  proposed substitute 
which includes: removal of the appraisal cost fee from the street/alley vacation by ordinance 
application, $2,500 limit for preliminary and final plat applications, $5,200 limit for expedited plat 
applications which is the sum of the preliminary and final plat applications plus a $200 fee and 
requirement for staff review of the fee schedule every 5 years. Mr. Bloo m noted he has received 
supportive feedback related to the proposed amendment.   Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom advised the 
last time the fee schedule was reviewed was October 25, 1993.  Appreciation was expressed to Mr. 
Bloom and his staff for review of the fee schedule.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes”– all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6213)

 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team 
(CRAFT) for Minimum Revenue Guarantee Payment (General Fund).  Wendy Volk, President of 
the Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team (CRAFT), provided information on the agreement 
noting the agreement should have been signed last summer , therefore, it  is retroactive.   She 
explained there was a delay with the runway project which ended up taking 4 months instead of 
11 weeks, and there was no air service during this period.  Ms. Volk a dvised 8,500 passengers 
have used air service since the runway project has been complete.  Ms. Volk provided further 
information on current scheduled flights and future proposed flights.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Volk 
explained the agreement will run through June  30, 2022  and 6 th Penny funds will start being 
collected June 1st.  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $240,000.00, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
(#7483) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Fire and Rescue 
Department, and UC Health c/o Cheyenne Medical Specialist to provide occupational medical 
exams (General Fund) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$50,000.00). (#7484) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Carbon Asset 
Network (CAN) establishing terms and conditions for land manager partnerships .  Dale 
Steenbergen, President and CEO of the Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, expressed 
continued support for the MOU, noting it is a great opportunity for community.  Mr. White moved 
to approve, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute  dated March 9, 
2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne explained the substitute removes  conflicting 
termination language, corrects typographical errors and adds greater liability to CAN specifically 
if the city is acting on information provided by CAN.  Upon inquiry, John Robitaille, CAN, advised 
at this time it is hard to predict income the city may receive as they are working on creating a new 
commodity and it depends on how much someone may be willing to pay for a ton of carbon stored 
in soil, but they are hoping to get $30/month per acre.  The city would then receive 2% of the fees 
collected. Mr. Robitaille noted currently there are 250,000 acres in network and they will continue 
marketing and increasing acreage over time. Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised it has not been 
determined which city department will administer the land manager partnerships. Comments were 
made in support of the concept and substitute and concern was expressed for how much 
involvement may be required by the city with only receiving 2% of collected fees.   Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Further 
comments were made in support of the MOU with hopes the process will continue to grow, the 
importance of the city’s participation and preference not to duplicate efforts that have already been 
done in areas such as the Belvoir Ranch with water and soil testing.  Main motion as amended by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception 
of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#7485) 
 
 Agreement between the Board of Public Utilities and Polo Ranch Company regarding 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision well development and associated water entitlement .  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Comment was made that the proposed agreement 
doesn’t contain any language that would prevent the developer from suing the city and there have 
been a number of lawsuits regarding the development and who is in control of the property.  Upon 
inquiry, Mayor Collins turned over duties of the Chair to City Council president Mr. Roybal and 
provided information on  the Polo Ranch.  Mayor Collins explained in 1955 the city bought the 
water rights and the ranch has been purchased by another Party and it has been attempted to rescind 
the ownership.  The p roperty was subdivided several years ago,  and several homes were given 
water illegally. Concern was expressed relating to the possibility of another lawsuit . Mr. Collins 
moved to postpone to March 28, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities, 
and McGee, Hearne & Paiz LLP for financial and compliance auditing services for fiscal the year 
ending June 30, 2022 (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.
