Renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, 
resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distiller y, special malt beverage and winery 
permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  Piper Rauzi, Poor Richard’s, provided an update on 
their business plan and the building at 1808 O’Neil Ave. where their retail liquor license is parked. 
Ms. Rauzi explained e nvironmental testing and building cleanout has occurred at the historic 
property.  The buildout will begin later this month which will include Phase I which will be a bar. 
She provided further information on the building explaining new garage doors have been installed 
through a DDA grant and it took a long time to get the doors in .  As the first allowed year period 
for parking the license is coming to an end, they w ould like to  request to park the license for 
another year but  expect the business to open in the next 4 -5 months.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Rauzi 
explained the business remodel will involve 2 phases – Phase I will be opening the front of the 
building as a bar and Phase II will be involving the back part of the building to include a concert 
venue.  She no ted food will also be served in some capacity.  Comments were made related to 
concern of a retail liquor license being in a parked status for 2 years and not collecting any sales 
tax dollars when there are so many establishments seeking that type of license.  Mr. White moved 
to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. White moved to divide the question to consider separate 
action on the application for renewal of the retail liquor license for Poor Richard’s, seconded by 
Dr. Aldrich.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White.  
Voting “no” - Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave.  Mr. White 
moved to divide the question to consider separate action on the establishments which have not 
provided TIPS lists during routine compliance checks.  These establishments include: Eagle’s 
Nest; Big D Oil #29; Vino’s Wine & Spirits; Accomplice Beer Co.; The Plains Hotel; Redwood 
Lounge and Package Liquors; China Buffet; Four Winds, Inc. and Peppermill Ba r & Liquor, 
seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” - Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn.  Voting 
“no” – Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr.  Segrave 
and Mr. White.  Comment was made relating to non -compliance of certain establishment 
concerning TIPS training all of which have become compliant since the Finance Committee 
meeting. The request was expressed that next year all establishments be able to provide TIPS 
training information when it is first requested  during Police Department compliance checks.   
Further comment was made relating to how liquor licenses have been issued over the years  and 
that some establishments initially issued retail licenses may be able to utilize bar and grill or 
restaurant liquor licenses and how it would be economically beneficial to be able to review these 
scenarios.  Main Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  [Clerk’s 
note: Mr. Esquibel joined the meeting via Zoom at 7:35 p.m. during discussion of this item] 
 
Transfer of ownership and location of retail liquor license application filed with the City 
Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas limited liability company d/b/a King Soopers, 
3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Avanti, Inc., d/b/a Dell 
Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Mr. White advised there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Teresa Dietz, Adult Beverage Specialist, 
King Soopers, explained that along with extending their existing space for the package liquor store, 
they will be moving the grocery pick up location and will be able to increase pick up orders for 
the community.  She noted all liquor store sales will take place in the designated space which will 
also include some groceries.  Justin Flohr, Store Manager of Cheyenne King Soopers, expressed 
appreciation for consideration of the license transfer and noted the transfer will allow for the 
possibility of career advancements for store associates.   Mr. White moved to approve, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Comments were made on the following topics: concern with alcohol sales in 
grocery stores; hi gh demand for retail liquor licenses; the perception that the King Soopers 
business isn’t in dire need of the license; other similar businesses that have received retail liquor 
licenses locally; lack of available liquor licenses at city and state levels; e conomic need for 
different business types such as a restaurant or unique bar to hold a retail liquor license; history of 
license as originally issued to Avanti’s for a small specialty wines package store and some citizen 
support for being able to purchase alcohol at a grocery store.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Stefanie 
Boster upon inquiry  advised the governing body  isn’t bound by precedence in their decision  to 
approve or deny the  transfer.  Ms. Boster referred to case law and state statutes, noting certain 
guidelines must be followed and evidence should be considered when they are reviewing the 
transfer.  Further comments were made regarding liquor sales in grocery/convenience stores and 
economic needs and growth.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Boster explained that since the renewal was 
approved for Dell Range Liquor, if the transfer isn’t approved, the license will remain with Dell 
Range Liquor.  It was further noted that there likely won’t be many businesses that will be able to 
purchase a license at market rate.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn and Mr. Segrave.  Voting “no” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and 
Mr. White.  City Clerk Kris Jones noted for the record  that she received a text at 7:35  p.m. that 
Mr. Esquibel had joined the meeting via Zoom during liquor license renewals discussion.   Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Boster explained pursuant to Roberts Rule of Order, a member can arrive late , and 
she believes Mr. Esquibel’s vote is appropriate. 
             
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 Central Ave nue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (license currently held by Dillinger’s, LLC d/b/a Dillinger’s, 1601 Central Ave., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 28 , 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Wyoming Horse Racing LLC  d/b/a Horse Palace, 1601 Central Ave nue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (license held by Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 Central Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
 
 Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held March 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym 
(LCSD #1 Boar d Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Osterfoss 
Living Trust Dated May 20, 2020 d/b/a Nagel-Warren Mansion, LLC, 222 E. 17th St., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming. 
 
 Under other business:   
 
 Jim Ridgeway expressed concern relating  to the city’s surplus sale of Cheyenne Police 
Department evidence seized firearms requirements.  TJ Barttlebort, Purchasing Manager, 
explained he had spoken to Mr. Ridgeway and provided information on the surplus sale noting 2 
bids are currently being sol icited for evidence seized firearms .  He advised it is the Police 
Department’s request to award the bid to someone residing more than 100 miles away from 
Cheyenne and also to one person submitting the highest responsive bid for all firearms.   Mayor 
Collins noted the bid will come before the Governing Body for approval.  Mr. Barttlebort noted 
he will have further discussions with the Police Department and that there is time to amend process 
if needed.  
 
 Judi Just commented on the city’s consideration of goi ng to a self-insured insurance plan 
and explained a situation previously when the city was self -insured and suffered great financial 
loss due to the plan. She provided information on recent legislative bills that if passed, could have 
negatively affected self-insured plans.  
 
 Mr. White noted the motion to adopt the following Resolution - Authorizing the Mayor 
and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, 
Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of 
the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive 
west and adjacent to Swee tgrass Drive), failed at the February 28, 2022 City Council meeting.  
Having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to Chapter 2.04 of City Code, Mr. White moved 
to reconsider the vote taken on the resolution, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Casey Palma, Palma Land 
Planning, agent for the owner, advised information on the development was distributed to members 
of the governing body.  Mr. White explained that following subsequent meetings with the agent 
and developers of the project, his concerns regarding parking and density of the project have been 
alleviated and requested support for the motion to reconsider.  Mr. White further explained that he 
is no longer concerned about parking and believes it will be sufficient.   Comment was made that 
there has been no additional information since the final vote was taken, the area remains limited, 
the original plan had an acre of open space  and that this plan has very little open space.   Mr. 
Palma, upon inquiry, advised that code would require 174 parking spaces and 68 bi ke parking 
spaces and that the plan has been increased from what code would require.  He noted the units will 
be available at current market rate.  Comments were made that following a tour of the property, 
the project is something that can be compared to other developments of this nature in the city and 
to take into consideration the entire project, open space and park land involved and that 
development projects are essentially to make money for  the developer.  Motion to reconsider 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Segrave 
voting “no”.  Dr.  Rinne moved to postpone for 2 weeks, seconded by Laybourn.  Dr. Rinne 
explained that historically when considering a reconsideration of an agenda item, it has been 
customary to postpone the item to allow for interested parties to become aware and have an 
opportunity to participate in the discussion.  It was noted that this item has had 5 public hearings 
and those who have want ed to comment have had the opportunity to do so.  Motion to postpone 
failed.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White.  Voting 
“yes” – Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal.    Upon inquiry 
from Mayor Collins, City Clerk Kris Jones read the Resolution entitlement into the record.  Mr. 
Palma provided clarification from a previous discussion on open space and noted there is a private 
park with amenities.  Upon inquiry, James Powers, representative of the applicant and realtor with 
Caldwell Banker, advised the developer is local and expressed appreciation for reconsidering the 
item.  Mr. Powers further noted that Summit Management sets a precedent for developers, the city 
has put together an affordable task force for 3,000 housing units for short term rentals, rents are 
climbing and will continue to do so  and that this project will benefit the community.  He advised 
the proposal is a reduction of 30% in density and that he has worked with city staff to bring a 
project forward that meets approval.  It was noted the developer is not the original family, that the 
governing body is not opposed to growth, but they do want quality growth.  Concern was raised 
on adequate parking for the project.  Mr. Powers provided examples of previous developments 
such as Carter Cottages with a less acceptable parking ratio.  He advised that the property owner 
is participating in and supports the project.  Chris Lovas, Lovas Engineering, advised the applicant 
has invested in the engineering plans which allows them to work with BOPU and city engineering 
staff to ensure  the project’s success from an engineering standpoint.  Additional comment was 
made that market rate for the proposed development units may not be affordable and caution was 
raised regarding the housing shortage and affordable housing.  Mr. Palma, upon inquiry, confirmed 
that proposed parking and open space meets current UDC requirements.  It was noted that if the 
development wasn’t in a PUD , it would have been approved  administratively.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no  
 
 Dr. Aldrich advised there will be a City Council Ward III Council and Coffee event on 
Saturday, March 19th, from 9-11 a.m. at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing Blvd.) 
 
 Mr. Esquibel apologized for failing to notify  members of the governing body  of his late 
arrival.  
 
 Mayor Collins requested the Governing Body meet  in executive session to discuss 
litigation matters and a proposed facility purchase.  Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session 
pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16 -4-405(a)(iii)(vii)(x), seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present.  Mayor Collins announced members 
would meet in executive session, not open to the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, 
City Treasurer and Chief of Staff be in attendance and stated there would be no action  taken by 
the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting 
would officially conclude at the close of the executive session. Mayor Collins further advised that 
the following staff members would be present in executive session respective to topics relating to 
their departments/divisions:  Compliance Director, Public Works Director, Transit Director, Fire 
Chief and the Fire Marshal. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 8:47 p.m.).  
  
 Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 9:48 p.m. 
 
 There being  no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:48 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
  
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  March 23, 2022

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-03-28 | CLIP_ID: 843 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 843

      
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 28, 2022 
  
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave.,  and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, and Tom Segrave.  Absent were: 
Pete Laybourn and Jeff White . Also present: Kristina F. Jones , City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
  
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve , seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 14, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
      Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 
Central Ave nue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Dillinger’s, LLC d/b/a 
Dillinger’s, 1601 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming).   There being no comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the 
application to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Wyoming Horse Racing LLC d/b/a Horse Palace, 
1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license held by Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 
1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming). There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application 
to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Osterfoss Living Trust Dated May 20, 2020 d/b/a Nagel -Warren 
Mansion, LLC, 222 E. 17 th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Jas Barbe, Inn Keeper for Nagel -Warren 
Mansion, reported the mansion anticipates reopening in approximately 2 weeks.   Mr. Barbe 
requested consideration for approval and provided history of his Wyoming residency  and 
commented on the historic value of the mansion and his excitement in being a part of its continued 
service to the community.  Supportive comments were voiced regarding the reopening and general 
support for downtown businesses.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
  
  ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint .  Reporting for 
Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to April 11, 2022, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  It was 
noted that staff is working on the amendment language which is anticipated to be complete in the 
next 2 weeks.  Motion to postpone carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the go verning body 
present.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4423) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public District to CB Community Business 
for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30 .  
Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4424) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed -Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles 
Street.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes”- all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. Johnson moved to postpone to April 
11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Bob Wilson voiced support for the proposed postponement , 
and noted he is working with city staff on language amendments and expressed appreciation to 
Stefanie Boster, City Attorney, and council members Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson for the attention 
to his concerns.  Annette Williams expressed concern regarding the lack of suppor t she feels she 
received at the March 22, 2022, Public Services Committee meeting.  Ms. Williams explained she 
had requested to speak following completion of action taken on the committee agenda and was 
told it was not customary for council committee meeti ngs to receive public comment except for 
comment relating to items on the agenda in proper order and she questioned this practice.  It was 
noted that Public Services Committee Chairman Bryan Cook did allow Ms. Williams to speak as 
a courtesy.  Mayor Collins explained that the governing body allows for public comment on 
general city business at the end of every City Council meeting and that they are not required to 
and have not allowed for public comment periods at standing committees for many years.  Georgia 
Broyles, Youth Alternatives, questioned if there would be a process for School Resource Officers 
to ticket student s violating the ordinance.  It was noted the request would be looked into. Sam 
Kayne and Pastor Tom Perea expressed support of t he proposed postponement.  Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

 Agreement between the  Board of Public Utilities and Polo Ranch Company regarding 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision well development and associated water entitlement.  Hank Bailey 
advised he is on the Polo Ranch Company Board of Directors and has proposed language to satisfy 
legal terms of the agreement.  Mr. Bailey provided information relating to past and proposed water 
usage involving rural properties and future development of r esidential homes.  Levi Martin, area 
resident, voiced concerns involving the city relinquishing water rights, fairness and previously 
agreed upon policy terms.  Brad Brooks , BOPU Director, upon inquiry provided information 
involving a previous water usage study completed in Laramie County .  Mr. Brooks provided 
information relating to proposed water usage allowances and actual usage on the Polo Ranch and 
requirement of the ranch to pay for any overuse, noting the Polo Ranch water usage is monitored.  
Mr. Brooks reported a previous Supreme Court opinion that determined a use of .7 acre feet per 
minute for county residential properties and advised he feels that is a fair assessment.  It was noted 
that the water use of the proposed residential home development would not be metered.  Comment 
was made that the state allows for up to 25 acre feet per minute for rural wells.  Mr. Brooks further 
noted that the proposed assessment provides a savings to the aquafer over al l, noting support of 
the stipulations in this agreement. Information was provided regarding the 1955 agreemen t.  Mr. 
Johnson moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Johnson moved to amend  by adding 
#10 which states “the Polo Ranch Company hereby acknowledges the binding rulings of the 
Wyoming Supreme Court in their 1998 and 2003 opinions in litigation inv olving Polo Ranch 
Company and the  City of Cheyenne and hereby expressly agrees that the 1955 Drilling and Water 
Use Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and John and Marie Bell has been finally and fully 
adjudicated and that the Polo Ranch has no claims or causes of action against the City of Cheyenne 
based on that Agreement or it s implementation.”, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present .     Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook 
and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#7487) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities, 
and McGee, Hearne & Paiz LLP for financial and compliance auditing services for fiscal the year 
ending June 30, 2022 (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7488) 
 
 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Umpire’s Association to 
umpire and score the Recreat ion Division Adult Softball Leagues and Tournaments for 2022 
(Recreation Programs Fund) .   Mr. Johnson moved to approve, seconded by Mr . Segrave. Mr. 
Johnson voted no. (#7489) 
 
[CA] 2022-2023 Collective Labor Agreement  between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne 
Firefighters, Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#7490) 
 
 Development Agreement between t he City of Cheyenne and Laramie County School 
District Number One to outline responsibilities for public infrastructure improvements relating to 
the Coyote Ridge Elementary School Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Mr.  Johnson moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $177,000, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Planning & Development 
Director Charles Bloom advised the agreement allows for the city to contribute to funding for 
improvements on Carlson Road , a land swap for land located near South High School and 
Harmony Valley.  Mr. Bloom reported the city can as k for improvement reimbursement when 
adjacent land is developed in the future  and that when the land is developed, the developers will 
be required to pay the city for the road.  It was noted the city will recoup approximately $76,000 
for the land swap.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#7491)  
 
[CA] Sponsorship Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and HollyFrontier Cheyenne

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7492)

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-14-22 for furnishing two (2) new 2022 4WD 72” front - mount

Department of Homeland Security Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount 
not to exceed $71,318.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue 
Department, and Stryker, Portage, MI, for equipment maintenance services.  (General Fund) 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $59,388.75).

No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Appointments for Erin LeBlanc to the Board of Adjustment and Elizabeth Bales to the 
Historic Preservation Board.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.   Mayor 
Collins upon inquiry provided informat ion on the application and selection process for mayoral 
appointments to boards and commissions.  Mayor Collins noted that it is possible for individuals 
to serve on multiple community boards at the same time and that the application by Elizabeth Bales 
was not vetted by the Historic Preservation Board prior to his recommendation to appoint Ms. 
Bales to the board.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to  be held April 11, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY,  and via electronic conference 
meeting, to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent to purcha se property located at 1800 
Westland Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming for the purpose of relocating the Cheyenne Transit System 
Office and Transfer Station. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 11, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers,  2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY , and via electronic conference 
meeting, for one available retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office by 
Ace’s Range LLC d/b/a Ace’s Range LLC, 1505 Pioneer Ave., Big D Oil Co. d/b/a Big D Oil #56, 
4100 Whitney Rd., Billy Jacks - Wyoming LLC d/b/a Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport 
Parkway West, Suite 1, Capitol Cuisine LLC d/b/a Ike’s Bar & Grill, 122 W. 6 th St., Ful-O-Pep 
d/b/a The Old Cheyenne Elevator, 800 W. 21 st St., Get Bent, LLC d/b/a Get Bent, LLC, 707 W. 
Lincolnway, Maurice W. Brown d/b/a Town & Country Supermarket Liquors-North, 2120 Carey 
Ave., Space 1, Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC d/b/a Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC, 
Anderson Corner, 3 rd Filing, Lot 2, Block 1, Vitamin College Natural Food Markets, I nc. d/b/a 
Natural Grocers, 1851 Dell Range Blvd., Westby Edge LLC d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th 
St. and Wyoming Downs OTB 14 LLC d/b/a Wyoming Downs OTB 14, 1345 Dell Range Blvd., 
Cheyenne. 
 
 Under other business:   
 
 Annette Williams advised that she felt that she did not get the same treatment at the March 
22, 2022 P ublic Services Committee as other members of the public and felt disrespected.  Ms. 
Williams requested approval to hang military memorial banners in downtown and other areas of 
town during the Veteran’s Day holiday.  She noted that family members of fallen military would 
be paying for the banners and the brackets will be used if approved by the Board of Public Utilities.  
Ms. Williams inquired about amending the current UDC and City Code sign regulations to allow 
for banners to be hung during certain time frames.  Mayor Collins advised that unfortunately by 
allowing one group to hang  banners it opens up the possibility that the city would be required to 
allow any group to hang banners.  Mayor Collins advised groups such as “hate groups” that may 
offend others would be allowed to hang their banners and reported that other Wyoming towns have 
been sued relating to this issue.  Mayor Collins reported that he is looking into options to allow 
groups such as the military to be able to be honored in ways suggested by Ms. Williams.  Ms. 
Williams inquired about scooters randomly placed around town and if that was a code violation.  
City Attorney Stefanie Boster advised they are not in violation of right of way ordinances.    
 
 Jim Ridgeway commented on revisions made to recent bid inquiries involving city surplus 
firearms.  He voiced concerns relating to the city’s ability to sell firearms without serial numbers, 
absence of visual inspection either in person or by photographs and method of current storage  by 
the city.    
 
 Mayor Collins upon inquiry advised the Municipal Building repai rs are near completion 
and that there does not appear there were financial savings during staff’s absence from the building 
during the past 6 months.     
 
  Bob Wilson voiced support for the postponement on the ordinance relating to creating a 
new Section 9.01.010-Definitions of Title 9  Public Peace and Welfare and looking into code 
changes relating to community signage.    
 
 There being  no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:15 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
  
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-04-11 | CLIP_ID: 845 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 845

      
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 11, 2022 
  
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich,  Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal , Tom Segrave and Jeff White .  
Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
  
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of  [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda .  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda items #21(d) and #21(e) were removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Aldrich, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Laybourn 
and Mr. Johnson, agenda item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 28, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
      Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent 
to purchase property located at 1800 Westland Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming for the purpose of 
relocating the Cheyenne Transit System Office and Transfer Station.   There being no comments 
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a  public hearing for one available retail liquor license application 
filed with the City Clerk’s Office by Ace’s Range LLC d/b/a Ace’s Range LLC, 1505 Pioneer 
Ave., Big D Oil Co. d/b/a Big D Oil #56, 4100 Whitney Rd., Billy Jacks - Wyoming LLC d/b/a 
Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport Parkway West, Suite 1, Capitol Cuisine LLC d/b/a Ike’s Bar 
& Grill, 122 W. 6 th St., Ful-O-Pep d/b/a The Old Cheyenne Elevator, 800 W. 21 st St., Get Bent, 
LLC d/b/a Get Bent, LLC, 707 W. Lincolnway,  Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC d/b/a 
Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC, Anders on Corner, 3 rd Filing, Lot 2, Block 1, Vitamin 
College Natural Food Markets, Inc. d/b/a Natural Grocers, 1851 Dell Range Blvd., Westby Edge 
LLC d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th St. and Wyoming Downs OTB 14 LLC d/b/a Wyoming 
Downs OTB 14, 1345 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne.   Mayor Collins advised there have been 2  
previous work sessions held on the applications  and noted there would be a 3 -minute limit for  
public comments.  The following individuals and downtown business owners spoke in support of 
The Old Cheyenne Elevator project:  Derek Baker, Bria Ham mock, Piper Rauzi, Dave Tuebner, 
Mirna Rummels and Jill Lovato. Comments included: business owners are good stewards of the 
community, rehabilitation of the iconic space, project involvement with the Reed Avenue corridor 
project, how the project will support economic development, how the business plan will support 
food truck s and other small community businesses, time invested in t he project and that the 
business is family oriented.  Andy Rose, The Old Cheyenne Elevator, read a letter into record in 
support of The Old Cheyenne Elevator.  Mariah Kilmer, spoke in favor of the rail spur concept 
and in support of the application for Get Bent, LLC, which she feels will add to continued growth 
of the area.  Amber Cromwell , The Design Studio, representing Ace ’s Range  LLC, provided 
information on the business and stated the retail liquor license is the only type of license that the 
business can utilize, noting the business will be open by the end of the year.  Dallas Tyrrell, 
Peaches’ Package Liquor  & Bar, identified a poss ible temporary location for compliance with 
operational status until construction is complete.  Juan Coronado, co-owner of Los Conejos  and 
Micro Popup Concepts , presented a letter of recommendation for Get Bent, LLC , from Troy 
Guard, Tag Restaurant Group, who he has worked with in the past.  JB Kunz, Aces Range LLC, 
provided a letter of support  from Mike Le pore and reported the project  will bring business to  
downtown year-round.  Jeremy Bay, spoke about alcohol and gambling and provided statistics 
involving suicide ideations related to substance abuse and gambling issues related to the Wyoming 
Downs application.  Diana McNamara spoke in support of Get Bent, LLC, noting the importance 
of the project location  relating to the west edge and t he long his tory with the railroad.  April 
Brimmer Kunz, Ace’s Range LLC and former legislator, commented that all of the applicants are 
worthy of a liquor license .  Ms. Brimmer Kunz asked that the governing body stand united in  
supporting possible legislative changes  relating to liquor licensing.   Seth Stefanik, co-owner of 
Los Conejos and Micro Popup Concepts, provided history of his culinary experience, voiced 
support for Get Bent, LLC and recommended approval of their application and explained they will 
be able to utilize the catering aspect and provide culinary education opportunities  to the 
community.  Members of the governing body expressed appreciation to all applicants and noted 
the difficulty that will be faced when awarding the license.  It was noted the legislature has agreed 
to hear discussion on the topic of liquor licenses and regulations.  Additionally, it was confirmed 
WAM notified members that Cheyenne has support from Burns, Lingle and Casper . Chugwater 
also has interest in the topic of liquor licenses  as the small communities are also being affected.   
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor 
Collins closed the public hearing and referred the applications to Committee of the Whole. 
 
  ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint  (Postponed from 
March 28, 2022).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 
1, 2022, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Sarah Bargst en, BOPU Water Conservation Specialist, 
explained how the change will contribute to the importance of water conservation.  Concerns were 
voiced involving abandonment of landscaping plans.  Inquiry was mad e relating to enforcement 
of abandoned landscaping plans and maintenance of newly planted trees.  Charles Bloom, Planning 
and Development Director, explained that the ordinance contains a provision for abandonment of 
a common landscaping plan and noted that with approval, each plan is recorded.  He advised that 
in the event that a common landscaping plan is abandoned, an application for a new site plan can 
be submitted that will work with the development.  Mr. Bloom noted that with c urrent site plans 
once a l andscape plan is approved , there are provisions that allow for compliance  and provided 
information on offenses.  He provided further information on 2 types of abandonments  in which 
compliance enforcement can intervene.  Mr. Bloom, upon further inquiry, reported he is not aware 
of specific examples where complaints have gone straight to enforcement for landscaping, instead 
it was enforced at time of s ite plan review.  It was noted that violations have been handled on a 
complaint basis and a summary of complaints could be provided.  Comments were voiced 
involving lack of maintenance for landscaping plans, replacement of dead trees and desire to 
enforce applicable city code.  Mr. Bloom  explained the ordinance amendment impacts 80-acre 
sites and larger which have been identified as areas on the edge of town and advised this is where 
they anticipate the common landscaping plans will be put into place. Motion to amend by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4425)  
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed -Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles 
Street.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion 
carried. Voting “yes”- all members of the governing body. (#4426) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (Postponed from March 
28, 2022).  Bob Wilson noted he attended the recent  Public Services Committee meeting and  is 
supportive of amendments to the ordinance.  Mr. Cook advised the item failed at the Public 
Services Committee meeting.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Chief Mark Francisco, Cheyenne Police Department, explained definitions 
in the harassment section of city code present challenges relating to enforcement and advised the 
department would be satisfied with a clearer  definition of  assault and batter y.  City Attorney  
Stefanie Boster noted the issue was defining “harass” and proposed the following  amendments: 
under paragraph A, remove words “compliments and”, under paragraph B, subsection 2, correct 
state statute citation from subsection (d) to (b) and remove paragraph B, subsection 3 in its entirety.  
Ms. Boster further clarified upon inquiry that “harass” is being rem oved as it was invoking the 
stalking statute.  Appreciation was expressed to the Police Department for their assistance with the 
ordinance and to everyone involved in the discussions.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Boster advised 
the ordinance isn’t solely for  the benefit of the recently approved anti -harassment ordinance, but 
the ordinance did highlight the need for definition clarification.  Ms. Boster noted the ordinance  
clarifies the definition of assault and battery by directing law enforcement to state statutes.  It was 
further noted the ordinance adopts statute which covers all sections in Title 9.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel 
and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Repealing Ordinance No. 3465, and reestablishing and 
redefining ward boundaries within the City of Cheyenne.   Jordan Evans, Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Coordinator, explained the reestablishment and redefinition of ward boundaries is to 
align with recent legislative redistricting changes and noted proposed ward boundary alterations.  
Mr. Evans advised he is working closely with the Laramie County Clerk to update precincts which 
is based off of the proposed ward changes.   Boyd Wiggam referenced an email he sent to the 
governing body requesting the City to consider expanding to 4 – 5 wards which he believes would 
allow the local government to be closer to citizens.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Inquiry was made that if additional wards were approved and a council 
member was previously in Ward III and was then considered to be in a newly established ward in 
the middle of their term, would they be required to run again in the newly established ward.  City 
Attorney Stefanie Boster  explained information would be provided relating  to this scenario and 
advised the City and County Clerk s have been c areful to make sure council members are not 
displaced with respect to the ir current wards.  City Clerk Kris Jones advised she would research 
the issue of being moved to a different ward in the middle of a council member ’s term.  Inquiry 
was made relating t o altering ward boundaries outside of the established 10 -year timeframe.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Jim Ridgeway questioned the need to increase fees to cover equipment and maintenance 
needs and asked if there are other funding options to cover the expenses.  Vicki Nemecek, Public 
Works Director, advised ARPA funds have been requested for some maintenance needs,  
specifically for the Transfer Station roof  replacement and advised the increase for residential 
customers is 1%.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was suggested that the City look for alternate funding sources 
for items such as roof replacements.  Comments were made relating to previous OSHA issues and 
financial studies relating to suggested rate increases involving sanitation services.  Ms. Nemecek 
advised the financial plan for the solid waste system includes everything that needs to be paid for 
long-term including daily operations and costs for the landfill expansion.  Ms. Nemecek explained 
at the landfill end of life, cells must be  closed or monitored for 30 years .  Upon inquiry, Ms. 
Nemecek provided information on the following: prior waste hauling to Ault, CO and funding and 
maintenance for roof replacements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Increasing the compensation for the Mayor of the City 
of Cheyenne, commencing January 1, 2025.  Jim Ridgeway inquired if the proposed wage increase 
could be done through a public e lection process, noting he doesn’t think the mayor and council 
members should vote on their own raises.  Mayor Collins advised that if the ordinance is approved, 
it won’t go into effect until the next Mayor takes office.  City Attorney Stefanie Boster explained 
that because of our form of government (Mayor/Council), state statute requires that the governing 
body determine the pay.  It was noted that the mayor receives other forms of compensation such 
as insurance benefits and use of a city vehicle.  Mr. Ridgeway spoke in opposition of the raise due 
to salary and benefits.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  
It was noted that the governing body received information from WAM containing compensation 
comparisons for several  Wyoming cities.  Cheyenne is one of  few cities in the state that ha s a 
Mayor/Council form of government and compensation for city managers in other communities is 
much higher t han the wage of Cheyenne’s mayor.  Clarification was made that non -binding 
referendums cannot be placed on an official ballot pursuant to state statute .  Support was voiced 
for city employees ’ wages be taken care of prior to raising compensation for members of the 
governing body.  Mayor Collins advised the last mayor al salary increase was approved in 2006 
and took place in 2009.  Members of the governing body commented on the previous increases for 
mayor and the demands required of the mayor.  Further comment was made that in comparison, 
mayors and city managers provide much of the same dedication and responsibility.  City Treasurer 
Robin Lockman confirmed there are 3 directors currently with higher salaries  than the mayor.   
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. 
Aldrich voting “no” and Mayor Collins abstaining.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Increasing the compensation for members of the City 
Council of the City of Cheyenne and providing an effective date.  Mr. White moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  It was noted that the  last city council increase  was in 
2000.  Additionally, the  Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA)  completed a cost  of 
living wage analysis and the result for council members  suggested the increase should reflect a 
monthly wage of $1,867.00 which is comparable to the suggested $2,000.00.  Clarification was 
made that some members of council would not be eligible for the increase until 2024 and that the 
proposed increase equates to about half of what county commissioners make for a  comparable 
service.  Concern was raised that the increase could impact 3 of the 9 council members due to 
election terms should those individuals choose to run again.  Comments were made relating to the 
amount of time it takes to serve on the council.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White voting “no”.    
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AR Agricultural Residential to Harmony Valley 
Planned Unit Development (Ordinance #4274) for land located north of West College Drive and 
east of York Avenue – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 10.52.030, Parking Not to Obstruct 
Traffic, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 9.12.080, Electric Fences Prohibited, 
of Chapter 9.12, Offenses Against Property, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich 
and Mr. Johnson). 
 
 Following a brief recess, Mayor Collins reconvened the meeting at 8:07 p.m.   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyomi ng 
Department of Transportation, Highway Safety Program, Grant Funding for the City’s mobile  
D.U.I. Command Vehicle (Highway Safety Behavi oral Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6224) 
 
 RESOLUTION – For Governing Body support of Community forum(s) and other 
appropriate initiatives to encourage more kindness, civility, cooperation, and respectful dialogue.  
Sandra Cane read a letter into the record and voiced support for the resolution.  Amy Spieker spoke 
in favor of the resolution and encouraged the governing body to consider the historical nature of 
the issues and problems.  Bob Wilson  spoke in support of the resolution noting it suggests the 
governing body is interested in citizens coming together in efforts that would reduce divisiveness 
and create a safer environment fo r everyone. Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  
Dr. Aldrich, sponsor of the resolution, expressed appreciation for the council’s consideration and 
advised there was previous discussion on if the resolution should have instead been a declaration. 
Members of the governing body provided comments supporting the effort, promotion of continued 
community conversations, past efforts in 2018 on a similar compassionate resolution and social 
justice issues. Appreciation was expressed to the public for remaining respectful and positive of 
each other’s  views during the series of meeting s on th e topic.  It was noted  the Chamber of 
Commerce will also be working on these types of forums and communications.  Further comment 
was made relating to the resolution format and that since no action is required, a proclamation may 
have been more appropriate.  Mayor Collins referenced a proclamation making April fair housing 
month.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6225) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Directing City staff to repair the tree lawn area on Vandehei Avenue 
between Yellowstone Road and Stockman Street (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax fund and General Fund 
Reserves).  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by 
substitute dated April 6, 2022 , seconded by Mr.  Segrave.  It was noted the substitute  replaces 
General Fund Reserves in the fifth  “Whereas” clause re placing it  with “approval from the 
governing body”.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Mr. Segrave provided members of the governing body pictures of the walkway 
from Yellowstone to Stockman , and clarified that the city is responsible for maintenance of this 
area as confirmed in details of an April 4, 1991 memo and provided information on the history of 
the project.  The importance of giving proper attention to details relating to agreements and other 
documents committing the city involving maintenance responsibilities was noted.  Comments and 
concerns were raised relating to tree removal and replacement, landscaping maintenance and  the 
importance of having a drought resistant plan.  Appreciation was expressed for the  City Forester 
and Rooted in Cheyenne  for implementing appropriate soil uses for planting of specific 
landscaping species for successful life spans and  for the importance of g oing to a drip watering 
system instead of an underground system.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6226) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities 
Construction Specifications and Standard Drawings, 2014 Edition – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Nova Health Care Center for occupational 
medicine services (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent  Agenda) carried (in an amount not to 
exceed $11,000.00).  (#7496)  
 
[CA] Memorandum of Agreement with Arts Cheyenne for the installation of public art in the 
City Right of Way.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7497)   
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understand ing between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Frontier 
Days, Inc., for the use of City-owned land and buildings for parking, camping, and other activities 
during the 2022 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
(#7498)    
    
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Waste Management 
of Colorado, Inc. for the Recycling Services Project (Solid Waste Fund) .  Mr. White moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $200,000.00, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Vicki 
Nemecek, Public Works Director , provided details on the city’s recycling program  including: 
hauling to Ft. Collins, p aying per ton based tonnage costs, and history and costs relating to 
contamination rates . Concerns were voiced involving the importance of a public outreach 
campaign to provide education  on what materials are recyclable  on an on -going basis.  Upon 
inquiry related to informational stickers on recycling bins, Ms. Nemecek noted replacement 
stickers would be included in rates and she doesn’t recommend replacing them.  Ms. Nemecek, 
upon inquiry, provided information on glass recycling and the recommendation not to place #4 
and #5 plastics in recycling bins.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. (#7499) 
 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and V & M Properties, LLC 
for property located at 1800 Westland Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the purpose of relocating 
the Cheyenne Transit System office and transfer station (Federal Grant WY -2021-005-00 and 
Transit Fund).  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $1,180,000.00, seconded 
by Dr. Aldrich.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, provided project history which included 
searching for an alternative location, noting the Transit Program was approached 2 years ago about 
relocating out of the downtown area.  She advised the proposed location will adequately meet the 
program’s need and reported a federal grant for a portion of the purchase was previously approved.  
Ms. Nemecek provided information on the process of acquiring the building  and expressed 
appreciation to Renea Jording, Transit Director, and her staff for their efforts involving the project.  
The grant amount of $1.3 million and a $260,000.00 grant match requirement was confirmed.  City 
Treasurer Robin Lockman, upon inquiry, confirmed the required grant match funding is available 
for the project.     Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7500)  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Engineeri ng & 
Consulting Alliance for the Landfill Tent Building deconstruction and replacement (Solid Waste 
Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $159,300.00).  (#7501) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Martin/ Martin 
Wyoming, Inc. for the Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil, Bridge Design, and

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-8-22 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Pine Bluffs 
Gravel & Excavating for Yard Waste Material Hauling and Fill Dirt Hauling for the City of

amount not to exceed $144,800.00). (#7502) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-19-22 for furnishing 95 Gallon Automated Refuse Carts for the 
City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(accepted bid from Otto Environmental Systems, Charlotte, NC, in an amount not to exceed 
$38,539.20). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-15-22 for furnishing two (2) new small articulated loaders and 
one (1) new heavy duty vehicle for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events 
Department (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax Funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-22-22 for furnishing three (3) new infield groomers for the 
Community Recreation - Events Department – Parks Division (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax Funding) 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Request to approve an additional dispensing area for City of Ch eyenne Limited Retail 
Liquor Licensee Cheyenne Country Club, located at 800 Stinner Rd., Cheyenne, Wyomin g.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office 
for Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license 
currently held by Dillinger’s, LLC d/b/a Dillinger’s, 1601 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming) .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office 
for Wyoming Horse Racing LLC d/b/a Horse Palace, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(license held by Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 Central Ave nue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming).  Paul Ryder, Wyoming Horse Racing LLC, explained he was first attracted to  the 
former Grier Building due to the location and the possibility of utilizing the retail liquor license .  
Mr. Ryder provided further information on the following: other Horse Palace locations with bars 
rarely serve as drinking establishment destinations without participating in gaming, staff will be 
tips trained, tax revenues that the business will generate, jobs generated by the busin ess and 
parking concerns.  He reported they intend to be good partners to downtown and have joined the 
Chamber of Commerce and will be joining other organizations.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Ryder provided 
information on their business demographic and reported that he does reside in Laramie  county.  
The following individuals spoke in opposition  of the transfer of ownership application :  Susan 
Allen, Hawthorne Tree, Katy Brady, downtown business owner, Amy Sp ieker, Laramie County 
Community Partnership, Robin Roling, Chief Officer for CRMC, Chris Brady and Jim Ridgeway.  
The following topics were noted in relation to opposition:  public safety, city’s public safety budget 
expenses, gambling and alcohol concerns, downtown homeless population and previous denial of 
an off-track betting establishment zoning application.  Dan Surdam, co -owner of Prime Realty , 
reported he is  passionate about downtown and noted that w ithout viable tenants, historical 
buildings often fall into disrepair.  Mr. Surdam commented that off track betting isn’t uncommon 
and acknowledged that alcohol and gambling addictions are real concerns, but he feels that denying 
the transfer isn’t the answer  to solving the overall issues.   Mr. White reported there was no 
recommendation from the Finance Committee.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  
Johnson.  Clarification was provided that a previous off track betting establishment was denied 
approval at the old Jackson’s location due to a zoning issue rather than eligibility of business type.  
It was noted that the Horse Palace can still open for business at the proposed location without a 
liquor license.  Upon inquiry, Chief Mark Francisco, Police Department, provided a history of calls 
to the existing Wyoming Horse Palace noting there have been a few but the  business has worked 
with the Police Department to improve security.    Upon inquiry, Mr. Ryder advised they do not 
plan on catering, the annual projection of alcohol sales is $128,000.00, employees will have health 
insurance and a 401k, they intend to hire approximately 1 0 employees and he provided proposed 
hours of operation.  It was further advised that should issues with th e establishment arise during 
the year, the y can be addressed  anytime, not just during the liquor license renewal period.  City 
Clerk Kris Jones , upon inquiry , reported that although Dillinger’s at one time inquired about 
operating as a steakhouse and subsequently had inquired about other business plans, they remained 
eligible for a retail liquor license.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Jones advised the transaction amount 
between license seller and buyer is a private matter.  Comments were made on the following: liquor 
license transfer s should be considered individually and specific to the business plan, gaming 
business locations in town, and concerns and history of failed businesses at the proposed location.  
Clarification was made that bars can  have games in their establishments and a few downtown 
locations were identified as having gaming machines.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Osterfoss Living 
Trust Dated May 20, 2020 d/b/a Nagle -Warren Mansion, LLC, 222 E. 17 th St., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to  be held April 25, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY,  and via electronic conference 
meeting, to hear public comment for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan Fiscal Year 2022 
prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office.      
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to  be held April 25, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY,  and via electronic conference 
meeting, to receive comments and input for an amendment to the Hitching Post Urban Renewal 
Plan and Project as required by Wyoming Statute Pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15-9-109. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to  be held April 25, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY,  and via electronic conference 
meeting for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s 
Office for 307 Horse Racing, Inc. d/b/a The Derby Club - Cheyenne, 1510 E. Pershing, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (license currently held by Stults Management, LLC d/b/a The Office Bar & Grill, 1600 
E. Pershing Blvd.). 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held A pril 25, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting for a for a bar and grill license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Stults 
Management LLC d/b/a The Office Bar & Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd. #100, Cheyenne, WY. 
 Under other business:   
 
 Dr. Aldrich provided information on the next City Council and Coffee event to be held on 
Saturday, April 16th from 9-11 a.m. at Dazbog Coffee, 3911 E. Pershing Blvd., Suite A. 
 
 Mr. Johnson spoke in support of the recent Stagecoach Motel purchase by the Comea 
Shelter, noting they recently closed on the property.  Mr. Johnson provided information on how 
the property will be utilized explaining it will be a controlled, gated environment and that their 
priority is resident safety. 
 
 Mr. Segrave expressed appreciation for being back in City Council chambers. 
 
 There being  no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:44 p.m.   
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
  
        Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2022-04-25 | CLIP_ID: 847 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 847

      
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 25, 2022 
  
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich,  Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Absent: Dr. Mark 
Rinne.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
  
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the design ation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda .  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 11, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
      Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to hear public comment for the City of Cheyenne’s 
Annual Action Plan Fiscal Year 2022 prepared by the C heyenne Housing and Community 
Development Office.  Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development, provided a brief 
overview of the history and purpose of the Community Development Block Grant program, noting 
the proposed plan is consistent with the 5 -year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan  completed in 
2020, and stated the anticipated allocation funding amount is $485,401.00 and $123,504.00 in 
recaptured funding.  Ms. Widauf reviewed informa tion on the grant application process ; public 
notifications and opportunities for input ; role of the Advisory Council in reviewing and 
recommending projects to be funded and explained the projects that are recommended for funding.  
Dr. Aldrich recognized  the attendance of Housing and Community Development Advisory 
Council Chair Wendy Soto and expressed appreciation for those who work with  and are a part of 
the council.  There being no  further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for an amendment 
to the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project as required by Wyoming Statute Pursuant to 
Wyoming Statute §15 -9-109.  Mark Christ ensen, Planning and Development Department, 
provided information on the amendment which updates tax increment funding (TIF) and includes 
drainage and detention facilities.  He a dvised the original plan was approved in late 2021 and 
provided information on what is allowed under the plan.  He further noted the amendment is largely 
due to a proposed hotel in the area.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a p ublic hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of 
ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for 307 Horse 
Racing, In c. d/b/a The Derby Club - Cheyenne, 1510 E. Pershing, Cheyenne, W Y (license 
currently held by Stults Management, LLC d/b/a The Office Bar &  Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing 
Blvd.). Kyle Ridgeway, 307 First, explained his businesses is the parent company of 307 Horse 
Racing, Inc. and responsible for redevelopment of the Pershing Plaza and provided information on 
the background of the retail liquor license. Mr. Ridgway explained 307 Horse Racing, Inc. has met 
Planning and Development and zoning requirements for the proposed location.   He further 
provided information on the business which will include a bar and off track betting machines on 
the first floor and the next phase will include different types of businesses in the upper level of the 
building which may include an arcade , axe throwing, etc.  Mr. Ridgeway noted they h ave been 
involved in multiple communities in the state .  There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the 
application to the Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Collins opened a p ublic hearing to receive comments and input for a bar and grill 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Stults Management LLC d/b/a The Office 
Bar & Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd. #100, Cheyenne, WY.  Kyle Ridgeway, speaking on behalf 
of The Office Bar & Eatery , advised the business is  transferring ownership of their  retail liquor 
license and will instead utilize a bar and grill license for the establishment.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public 
hearing and referred the application to the Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal. Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Mayor Collins advised the substitute removes the definit ion of harassment.  Motion to amend by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.   Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins explained the purpose of the substitute 
is to remove the definition of harassment because it evoked stalking laws per state statute.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the 
exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#4427) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Repealing Ordinance No. 3465, and reestablishing and 
redefining ward boundaries within the City of Cheyenne.   Jordan Evans, Coordinator of GIS 
Cooperative, provided information on the boundary changes noting the city and county have 
worked together on the boundary changes.  Boyd Wiggam, advocated for the Council to consider 
expanding the number of w ards, referenced  a Wyoming Tribune -Eagle editorial which  also 
recommended expanding wards and noted results of a poll where a  majority of people that  
participated agreed that wards should be expanded.  Mr. Wiggam requested that if there is adequate 
interest for expansion of wards, to not advance the ordinance and let a process through the county 
take place.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. 
Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 18, 2022, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mayor Collins 
explained the substitute includes actual survey language that was previously not included and that 
the map didn’t change.  Comment was made that constituents are interested in expanding wards , 
however due to timing constraints, it is not conducive at this time.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present.  Upon inquiry, Mr.  Evans 
provided current population amounts for each ward using census data and noted the counts do not 
include anyone living in the Sweetgrass development.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4428) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Reporting for Fi nance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#4429) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Increasing the compensation for the Mayor of the City 
of Cheyenne, commencing January 1, 2025.   Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no” and Mayor Collins who abstained. 
(#4430)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Increasing the compensation for members of the City 
Council of the City of Cheyenne and providing an effective date.  Mr. White moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook and Mr. White voting 
“no”. (#4431)    
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AR Agricultural Residential to Harmony Valley 
Planned Unit Development (Ordinance #4274) for land located north of West College Drive and 
east of York Avenue.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 10.52.030, Parking Not to Obstruct 
Traffic, of Chapt er 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 9.12.080, Electric Fences Prohibited, 
of Chapter 9.12, Offenses Against Property, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 10.52.230 Downtown 
Construction/Maintenance Worker Parking in an Established Timed  Parking Zone, of Chapter 
10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of 
the City of Cheyenne – referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
   
 RESOLUTION – Amending the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities 
Construction Specifications and Standard Drawings, 2014 Edition .  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 9, 2022, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave.  Frank Strong, Board of Public Utilities, explained the substitute is to update the 
water meter pit detail  drawing which is included in what is required for installation.   Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present.  Main

an amount not to exceed $185,700.00 , seconded by Mr . Roybal.  Comments and concerns were

many unknowns including bridge location  in conjunction with existing railroad tracks, Belvoir

relating to agreement and budget allocation, agreement with railroad and reference to an email
